2025-06-16T22:05:54.8315928Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8316142Z <pr_title>
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8316985Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8317808Z </pr_title>
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8318028Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8318199Z <pr_description>
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8319773Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8321299Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8321602Z ## Problem
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8322281Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8323200Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8323670Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8324159Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8324651Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8325131Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8325531Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8325744Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8327035Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8328061Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8328198Z ## Solution
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8328392Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8328787Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8329369Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8329839Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8330522Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8332012Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8333190Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8333719Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8333883Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8334369Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8334692Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8334847Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8335210Z ```bash
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8335913Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8336718Z ```
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8336827Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8336924Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8337152Z ```bash
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8337817Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8338580Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8338819Z ```
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8338924Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8339071Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8339323Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8339417Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8340013Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8340546Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8340629Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8340892Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8341390Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8341919Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8342410Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8342994Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8343316Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8343729Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8344218Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8344294Z Fixes #11.

2025-06-16T22:05:54.8514539Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8515028Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8515144Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8515353Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8515445Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8515945Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8516610Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8517088Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8517653Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8518006Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8518501Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8518934Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8519025Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8519311Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8519661Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8519977Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8520193Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8520288Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8521284Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8522222Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8522308Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8523012Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8523692Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8524091Z Let me know once you've completed the OAuth client setup and added the GitHub secrets, and I'll activate the Tailscale integration.

2025-06-16T22:05:54.8529404Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8529684Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530075Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530165Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530314Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530535Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530863Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8530945Z ```json
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8531121Z {
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8531296Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8531515Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8531788Z   },
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8531961Z   "acls": [
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8532139Z     {
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8532325Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8532552Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8532777Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8532983Z     }
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533152Z   ]
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533324Z }
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533492Z ```
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533591Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533685Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8533823Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8534116Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8534636Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8535154Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8535804Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8536010Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8536107Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8536462Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8536940Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8537794Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8538022Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8538514Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8538957Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8539254Z 
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8539797Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
2025-06-16T22:05:54.8540509Z </comment_old>

2025-06-16T22:06:27.8463985Z               - heading "Navigate to header Mini-history of Prefect" [level=2] [ref=e218]:
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8464567Z                 - link "Navigate to header" [ref=e220] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8464931Z                   - /url: "#mini-history-of-prefect"
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8465351Z                   - img [ref=e222] [cursor=pointer]
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8465721Z                 - generic [ref=e224] [cursor=pointer]: Mini-history of Prefect
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8466292Z               - paragraph [ref=e225]:
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8466577Z                 - strong [ref=e226]: "2018-2021:"
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8469438Z                 - text: Our story begins in 2018, when we introduced the idea that workflow orchestration should be Pythonic. Inspired by distributed tools like Dask, and building on the experience of our founder, Jeremiah Lowin (a PMC member of Apache Airflow), we created a system based on simple Python decorators for tasks and flows. But what made Prefect truly special was our introduction of task mapping—a feature that would later become foundational to our dynamic execution capabilities (and eventually imitated by other orchestration SDKs).
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8471736Z               - paragraph [ref=e227]:
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8472108Z                 - strong [ref=e228]: "2022:"
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8474569Z                 - text: "Prefect’s 2.0 release became inevitable once we recognized that real-world workflows don’t always fit into neat, pre-planned DAG structures: sometimes you need to update a job definition based on runtime information, for example by skipping a branch of your workflow. So we removed a key constraint that workflows be written explicitly as DAGs, fully embracing native Python control flow—if/else conditionals, while loops-everything that makes Python…Python."
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8476797Z               - paragraph [ref=e229]:
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8477103Z                 - strong [ref=e230]: "2023-present:"
2025-06-16T22:06:27.8478816Z                 - text: With our release of Prefect 3.0 in 2024, we fully embraced these dynamic patterns by open-sourcing our events and automations backend, allowing users to natively represent event-driven workflows and gain additional observability into their execution. Prefect 3.0 also unlocked a leap forward in performance, improving the runtime overhead of Prefect by up to 90%.

2025-06-16T22:07:30.0994148Z     66.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0994596Z     67.Once installed, you can use Prefect in your OT-2 scripts:
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0995181Z     68.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0995480Z     69.```python
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0995834Z     70.from prefect import flow, task
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0996478Z     71.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0996812Z     72.@task
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0997178Z     73.def prepare_plate():
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0997488Z     74.    print("Preparing plate...")
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0998057Z     75.    # Your OT-2 plate preparation code here
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0998395Z     76.    return "Plate ready"
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0998642Z     77.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0998817Z     78.@task  
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0999145Z     79.def run_protocol(plate_status):
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0999481Z     80.    print(f"Running protocol with {plate_status}")
2025-06-16T22:07:30.0999819Z     81.    # Your OT-2 protocol code here
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1000104Z     82.    return "Protocol completed"
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1000355Z     83.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1000527Z     84.@flow
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1000720Z     85.def ot2_workflow():
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1000961Z     86.    plate = prepare_plate()
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1001228Z     87.    result = run_protocol(plate)
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1001521Z     88.    print(f"Workflow finished: {result}")
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1001928Z     89.    return result
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1002145Z     90.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1002327Z     91.# Run the workflow
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1002558Z     92.if __name__ == "__main__":
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1002809Z     93.    ot2_workflow()
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1003015Z     94.```

2025-06-16T22:07:30.1004390Z     101.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1004654Z     102.1. **Check Python version**: Ensure you're using Python 3.10
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1004997Z     103.   ```python

2025-06-16T22:07:30.1005439Z     105.   ```

2025-06-16T22:07:30.1007320Z     111.   ```
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1007506Z     112.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1007705Z     113.3. **Check wheel compatibility**:
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1007976Z     114.   ```python
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1008321Z     115.   python -c "import platform; print('Architecture:', platform.machine())"
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1008739Z     116.   ```
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1009027Z     117.   Should show `armv7l` for OT-2
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1009289Z     118.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1009470Z     119.### Download Issues

2025-06-16T22:07:30.1017675Z     148.## System Requirements
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1018002Z     149.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1018219Z     150.- **Architecture**: ARMv7l (Raspberry Pi 3B+)
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1018524Z     151.- **Python**: 3.10+
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1018768Z     152.- **OS**: Opentrons custom firmware
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1019092Z     153.- **Network**: Internet access for initial download
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1019441Z     154.- **Storage**: ~20MB free space for all wheels
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1019727Z     155.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1019937Z     156.## Background
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1020136Z     157.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1020485Z     158.This solution addresses the complexity of installing Prefect on OT-2 systems where:
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1020912Z     159.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1021193Z     160.- Standard pip install fails due to compilation requirements
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1021633Z     161.- pendulum (Prefect dependency) requires Rust compilation 
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1022008Z     162.- ARMv7l wheels are not readily available
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1022443Z     163.- Limited system tools (no git, bash, venv)
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1022739Z     164.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1023132Z     165.The pre-built wheels in this repository eliminate these issues by providing compatible binaries.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1023605Z     166.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1023777Z     167.## Support
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1023967Z     168.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1024153Z     169.If you encounter issues:
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1024390Z     170.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1024618Z     171.1. Check that you're on an ARMv7l system (OT-2)
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1024949Z     172.2. Verify Python 3.10 is being used
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1025254Z     173.3. Ensure network connectivity to GitHub
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1025616Z     174.4. Try the automated installer first before manual methods
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1025942Z     175.
2025-06-16T22:07:30.1026507Z     176.For additional help, refer to the repository issues or documentation.

2025-06-16T22:07:41.9182758Z       ### Prefect vs Prefect-Client
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9183178Z       
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9183746Z       **For OT-2 systems, we recommend `prefect-client`** instead of the full `prefect` package:
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9184456Z       
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9185139Z       - **prefect-client**: Minimal installation (~803KB) designed for resource-constrained environments
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9186558Z       - **prefect**: Full installation (~5.8MB) with CLI and server components that may not be needed on OT-2
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9187381Z       
2025-06-16T22:07:41.9188561Z       The `prefect-client` package provides all the core workflow functionality you need for OT-2 automation while avoiding complex dependencies that can cause installation issues.

2025-06-16T22:10:49.6593353Z       ## Usage
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6593718Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6594373Z       The API is **identical** to full Prefect. Your existing Prefect code works without changes:
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6595169Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6595509Z       ```python
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6595919Z       from prefect import flow, task
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6596557Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6597132Z       @task
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6597549Z       def prepare_plate():
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6598033Z           print("Preparing plate...")
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6598567Z           return "Plate ready"
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6599195Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6599541Z       @task  
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6599943Z       def run_protocol(plate_status):
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6600535Z           print(f"Running protocol with {plate_status}")
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6601147Z           return "Protocol completed"
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6601639Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6601979Z       @flow
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6602358Z       def ot2_workflow():
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6602816Z           plate = prepare_plate()
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6603330Z           result = run_protocol(plate)
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6603843Z           return result
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6604250Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6604600Z       # Works exactly the same as full Prefect
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6605156Z       if __name__ == "__main__":
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6605640Z           ot2_workflow()
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6606061Z       ```
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6606583Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6606953Z       ## What's NOT Included
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6607396Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6608240Z       Since `prefect-client` is designed for connecting to remote Prefect servers, these components are not included:
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6609221Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6609789Z       - **Prefect CLI commands** (`prefect server start`, `prefect deploy`, etc.)
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6610670Z       - **Local Prefect server** (you can't run a server on the OT-2)
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6611476Z       - **Prefect UI/Dashboard** (use remote server for this)
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6612169Z       - **Development tools** (profile management, etc.)
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6612744Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6613116Z       ## When to Use Which Version
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6613592Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6613966Z       ### Use `prefect-client` if:
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6614696Z       - ✅ Running on OT-2 or resource-constrained systems
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6615441Z       - ✅ Connecting to remote Prefect Cloud/server
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6616168Z       - ✅ Only need workflow execution capabilities
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6617017Z       - ✅ Want minimal installation footprint
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6617571Z       
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6617946Z       ### Use full `prefect` if:
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6618536Z       - ❌ Need to run local Prefect server
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6619169Z       - ❌ Need CLI tools for development
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6619880Z       - ❌ Have plenty of resources and no space constraints
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6620608Z       - ❌ Need the full development environment
2025-06-16T22:10:49.6621152Z

2025-06-16T22:12:17.6115443Z   +# Prefect Client Solution for OT-2
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6115702Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6115867Z   +## Overview
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6116050Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6117241Z   +This document explains the **prefect-client** solution for installing Prefect on Opentrons OT-2 systems. After encountering compilation issues with the full Prefect package, we've implemented support for `prefect-client` - Prefect's official lightweight distribution.
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6118337Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6118523Z   +## What is Prefect Client?
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6118764Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6119118Z   +`prefect-client` is Prefect's minimal installation package designed specifically for:
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6119619Z   +- **Resource-constrained environments** (like OT-2)
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6120064Z   +- **Lightweight deployments** where full server components aren't needed
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6120552Z   +- **Embedded systems** with limited storage and computing resources
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6120901Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6121074Z   +### Key Differences
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6121277Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6121482Z   +| Feature | prefect-client | prefect (full) |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6121798Z   +|---------|---------------|---------------|
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6122080Z   +| **Size** | 803KB | 5.8MB |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6122419Z   +| **Dependencies** | Minimal core dependencies | Full server stack |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6123048Z   +| **CLI Tools** | ❌ Not included | ✅ Full CLI suite |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6123496Z   +| **Server Components** | ❌ Not included | ✅ Server, UI, etc. |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6123945Z   +| **Core Workflows** | ✅ Full support | ✅ Full support |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6124388Z   +| **Task/Flow Decorators** | ✅ Full support | ✅ Full support |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6124877Z   +| **Remote Server Connection** | ✅ Designed for this | ✅ Supported |
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6125235Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6125411Z   +## Benefits for OT-2
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6125618Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6125897Z   +1. **Smaller installation footprint** - 7x smaller than full Prefect
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6126609Z   +2. **Fewer dependencies** - Reduces compilation issues
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6127042Z   +3. **Same core functionality** - All workflow features work identically
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6127508Z   +4. **Official Prefect package** - Fully supported by Prefect team
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6127934Z   +5. **Designed for embedded use** - Perfect for OT-2 use cases
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6128261Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6128433Z   +## Installation
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6128627Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6128952Z   +### Quick Installation (Recommended)
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6129220Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6129385Z   +```python
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6129602Z   +# Download and run the automated installer

2025-06-16T22:12:17.6131364Z   +```
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6131534Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6131712Z   +### Manual Installation
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6131930Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6132097Z   +```python
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6132304Z   +# Method 1: Using our Python installer
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6132595Z   +python install.py pendulum

2025-06-16T22:12:17.6138522Z   +```
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6138689Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6138852Z   +## Usage
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6139024Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6139360Z   +The API is **identical** to full Prefect. Your existing Prefect code works without changes:
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6139782Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6139945Z   +```python
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6140161Z   +from prefect import flow, task
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6140406Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6140568Z   +@task
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6140752Z   +def prepare_plate():
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6140990Z   +    print("Preparing plate...")
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6141256Z   +    return "Plate ready"
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6141479Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6141641Z   +@task  
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6141836Z   +def run_protocol(plate_status):
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6142134Z   +    print(f"Running protocol with {plate_status}")
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6142452Z   +    return "Protocol completed"
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6142694Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6142860Z   +@flow
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6143039Z   +def ot2_workflow():
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6143269Z   +    plate = prepare_plate()
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6143519Z   +    result = run_protocol(plate)
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6143774Z   +    return result
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6143972Z   +
2025-06-16T22:12:17.6144166Z   +# Works exactly the same as full Prefect
